					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content mw-content-ltr" lang="en" dir="ltr"><div class="mw-parser-output"><div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none">Castilian Catholic priest and founder of the Dominican Order</div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">For the village in Cornwall, see <a href="/wiki/St_Dominic,_Cornwall" title="St Dominic, Cornwall">St Dominic, Cornwall</a>. For places named after Saint Dominic, or other saints named Dominic, see <a href="/wiki/St_Dominic_(disambiguation)" class="mw-disambig" title="St Dominic (disambiguation)">St Dominic (disambiguation)</a>.</div>
<p class="mw-empty-elt">
</p>

href="/wiki/Campana,_Calabria" title="Campana, Calabria">Campana, Calabria</a>, <a href="/wiki/Managua" title="Managua">Managua</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Saint Dominic</b>, <span class="noexcerpt nowraplinks" style="font-size:85%; font-weight:normal;"><a href="/wiki/Order_of_Preachers" class="mw-redirect" title="Order of Preachers">OP</a></span> (<a href="/wiki/Spanish_language" title="Spanish language">Spanish</a>: <i lang="es">Santo Domingo</i>; 8 August 1170 – 6 August 1221), also known as <b>Dominic de Guzmán</b> (<small>Spanish:&#160;</small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="es-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Spanish" title="Help:IPA/Spanish">[ɡuθˈman]</a></span>), was a <a href="/wiki/Castilians" title="Castilians">Castilian</a> <a href="/wiki/Catholic_priest" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholic priest">Catholic priest</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mysticism" title="Mysticism">mystic</a>, the founder of the <a href="/wiki/Dominican_Order" title="Dominican Order">Dominican Order</a> and is the <a href="/wiki/Patron_saint" title="Patron saint">patron saint</a> of <a href="/wiki/Astronomers" class="mw-redirect" title="Astronomers">astronomers</a> and <a href="/wiki/Natural_scientists" class="mw-redirect" title="Natural scientists">natural scientists</a>. He is alternatively called <b>Dominic of Osma</b>, <b>Dominic of Caleruega</b>, and <b>Domingo Félix de Guzmán</b>.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Life">Life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Dominic&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Birth_and_early_life">Birth and early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Dominic&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Birth and early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Dominic was born in <a href="/wiki/Caleruega" title="Caleruega">Caleruega</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-3op_1-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3op-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> halfway between <a href="/wiki/Osma" class="mw-redirect" title="Osma">Osma</a> and <a href="/wiki/Aranda_de_Duero" title="Aranda de Duero">Aranda de Duero</a> in <a href="/wiki/Old_Castile" title="Old Castile">Old Castile</a>, <a href="/wiki/Spain" title="Spain">Spain</a>. He was named after <a href="/wiki/Dominic_of_Silos" title="Dominic of Silos">Saint Dominic of Silos</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Benedictine" class="mw-redirect" title="Benedictine">Benedictine</a> <a href="/wiki/Abbey_of_Santo_Domingo_de_Silos" title="Abbey of Santo Domingo de Silos">abbey of Santo Domingo de Silos</a> lies a few miles north of Caleruega.
</p><p>In the earliest narrative source, by <a href="/wiki/Jordan_of_Saxony" title="Jordan of Saxony">Jordan of Saxony</a>, Dominic's parents are not named. The story is told that before his birth his barren mother made a pilgrimage to the <a href="/wiki/Abbey_of_Santo_Domingo_de_Silos" title="Abbey of Santo Domingo de Silos">Abbey at Silos</a>, and dreamt that a dog leapt from her womb carrying a flaming torch in its mouth, and seemed to set the earth on fire. This story is likely to have emerged when his order became known, after his name, as the <i>Dominican</i> order, <i>Dominicanus</i> in Latin and a play on words interpreted as <i>Domini canis</i>: "Dog of the Lord."<sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> Jordan adds that Dominic was brought up by his parents and a maternal uncle who was an archbishop.<sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> The failure to name his parents is not unusual, since Jordan wrote a history of the Order's early years, rather than a biography of Dominic. A later source, still of the 13th century, gives their names as <a href="/wiki/Juana_de_Aza" class="mw-redirect" title="Juana de Aza">Juana</a> and Felix.<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> Nearly a century after Dominic's birth, a local author asserted that Dominic's father was <i>"vir venerabilis et dives in populo suo"</i> ("an honoured and wealthy man in his village").<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> The travel narrative of <a href="/wiki/Pero_Tafur" class="mw-redirect" title="Pero Tafur">Pero Tafur</a>, written circa 1439 (about a pilgrimage to Dominic's tomb in Italy), states that Dominic's father belonged to the family <a href="/wiki/Dukes_of_Medina_Sidonia" class="mw-redirect" title="Dukes of Medina Sidonia">de Guzmán</a>, and that his mother belonged to the <i>Aça</i> or Aza family.<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> Dominic's mother, <a href="/wiki/Joan_of_Aza" title="Joan of Aza">Joan of Aza</a>, was beatified by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Leo_XII" title="Pope Leo XII">Pope Leo XII</a> in 1829.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education_and_early_career">Education and early career</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Dominic&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Education and early career">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:242px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Daniel_van_den_Dyck_-_St_Dominic_accompanied_by_Simon_de_Montfort_raising_the_crucifix_against_the_Albigensians.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Daniel_van_den_Dyck_-_St_Dominic_accompanied_by_Simon_de_Montfort_raising_the_crucifix_against_the_Albigensians.JPG/240px-Daniel_van_den_Dyck_-_St_Dominic_accompanied_by_Simon_de_Montfort_raising_the_crucifix_against_the_Albigensians.JPG" decoding="async" width="240" height="222" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Daniel_van_den_Dyck_-_St_Dominic_accompanied_by_Simon_de_Montfort_raising_the_crucifix_against_the_Albigensians.JPG/360px-Daniel_van_den_Dyck_-_St_Dominic_accompanied_by_Simon_de_Montfort_raising_the_crucifix_against_the_Albigensians.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Daniel_van_den_Dyck_-_St_Dominic_accompanied_by_Simon_de_Montfort_raising_the_crucifix_against_the_Albigensians.JPG/480px-Daniel_van_den_Dyck_-_St_Dominic_accompanied_by_Simon_de_Montfort_raising_the_crucifix_against_the_Albigensians.JPG 2x" data-file-width="810" data-file-height="749" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Daniel_van_den_Dyck_-_St_Dominic_accompanied_by_Simon_de_Montfort_raising_the_crucifix_against_the_Albigensians.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><i>A picture of St Dominic accompanied by Simon de Montfort raising the crucifix against the Cathars</i> by <a href="/wiki/Daniel_van_den_Dyck" title="Daniel van den Dyck">Daniel van den Dyck</a></div></div></div>
<p>At fourteen years of age, Dominic was sent to the <a href="/wiki/Premonstratensian" class="mw-redirect" title="Premonstratensian">Premonstratensian</a> monastery of <a href="/wiki/Santa_Mar%C3%ADa_de_La_Vid" class="mw-redirect" title="Santa María de La Vid">Santa María de La Vid</a> and subsequently transferred for further studies in the schools of Palencia.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> In Palencia, he devoted six years to the <a href="/wiki/Arts" class="mw-redirect" title="Arts">arts</a> and four to <a href="/wiki/Theology" title="Theology">theology</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuiraud19137_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuiraud19137-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> At some point in time he also joined Santa María de La Vid as a canon.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_7-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1191, when Spain was desolated by famine,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuiraud19137_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuiraud19137-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> young Dominic gave away his money and sold his clothes, furniture, and even precious manuscripts to feed the hungry. Dominic reportedly told his astonished fellow students, "Would you have me study off these dead skins when men are dying of hunger?"<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>At the age of 24, Dominic was ordained as a priest, and subsequently joined the <a href="/wiki/Canonry" class="mw-redirect" title="Canonry">canonry</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Burgo_de_Osma_Cathedral" title="Burgo de Osma Cathedral">Cathedral of Osma</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> In 1198, <a href="/wiki/Diego_de_Acebo" title="Diego de Acebo">Diego de Acebo</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Osma" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Osma">Bishop of Osma</a>, having reformed the chapter and assigned himself as prior, made Dominic the subprior of the chapter.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_7-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1203 or 1204 he accompanied Diego de Acebo on a diplomatic mission for <a href="/wiki/Alfonso_VIII_of_Castile" title="Alfonso VIII of Castile">Alfonso VIII</a>, <a href="/wiki/King_of_Castile" class="mw-redirect" title="King of Castile">King of Castile</a>, to secure a bride in <a href="/wiki/Denmark" title="Denmark">Denmark</a> for crown prince Ferdinand.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> The envoys traveled to Denmark via <a href="/wiki/Aragon" title="Aragon">Aragon</a> and the south of <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a>. The marriage negotiations ended successfully, but the princess died before leaving for Castile.<sup id="cite_ref-3op_1-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3op-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> During their return journey, they met with <a href="/wiki/Cistercian" class="mw-redirect" title="Cistercian">Cistercian</a> monks who had been sent by Pope Innocent III to preach against the <a href="/wiki/Cathars" class="mw-redirect" title="Cathars">Cathars</a>, a Christian religious sect with gnostic and dualistic beliefs which the Catholic Church deemed heretical. Dominic and Diego de Acebo attributed the Cistercians' lack of success to their extravagance and pomp compared to the asceticism of the Cathars. They decided to adopt a more ascetic way of life and began a program in the south of <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a> to convert the Cathars.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_7-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> Catholic-Cathar debates were held at <a href="/wiki/Verfeil,_Haute-Garonne" title="Verfeil, Haute-Garonne">Verfeil</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pamiers" title="Pamiers">Pamiers</a> and <a href="/wiki/Montr%C3%A9al,_Aude" title="Montréal, Aude">Montréal</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> Diego de Acebo later died, leaving Dominic alone in his mission.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_10-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foundation_of_the_Dominicans">Foundation of the Dominicans</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Dominic&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Foundation of the Dominicans">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:7,_8_place_du_Parlement_Chapelle,_Toulouse.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/7%2C_8_place_du_Parlement_Chapelle%2C_Toulouse.jpg/220px-7%2C_8_place_du_Parlement_Chapelle%2C_Toulouse.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="280" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/7%2C_8_place_du_Parlement_Chapelle%2C_Toulouse.jpg/330px-7%2C_8_place_du_Parlement_Chapelle%2C_Toulouse.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/7%2C_8_place_du_Parlement_Chapelle%2C_Toulouse.jpg/440px-7%2C_8_place_du_Parlement_Chapelle%2C_Toulouse.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4912" data-file-height="6250" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:7,_8_place_du_Parlement_Chapelle,_Toulouse.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Saint Dominic's House in <a href="/wiki/Toulouse" title="Toulouse">Toulouse</a></div></div></div>
<p>In 1215, Dominic established himself, with six followers, in a house given by Peter Seila, a rich resident of <a href="/wiki/Toulouse" title="Toulouse">Toulouse</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuiraud191365–66_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuiraud191365–66-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Dominic saw the need for a new type of organization to address the spiritual needs of the growing cities of the era, one that would combine dedication and systematic education, with more organizational flexibility than either monastic orders or the secular clergy. He subjected himself and his companions to the <a href="/wiki/Monastic" class="mw-redirect" title="Monastic">monastic</a> rules of <a href="/wiki/Prayer" title="Prayer">prayer</a> and <a href="/wiki/Penance" title="Penance">penance</a>; <a href="/wiki/Folquet_de_Marselha" title="Folquet de Marselha">Bishop Foulques</a> gave them written authority to preach throughout the territory of Toulouse.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Also in 1215, the year of the <a href="/wiki/Fourth_Lateran_Council" class="mw-redirect" title="Fourth Lateran Council">Fourth Lateran Council</a>, Dominic and Foulques went to Rome to secure the approval of the Pope, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Innocent_III" title="Pope Innocent III">Innocent III</a>. Dominic returned to Rome a year later, and was finally granted written authority in December 1216 and January 1217 by the new pope, <a href="/wiki/Honorius_III" class="mw-redirect" title="Honorius III">Honorius III</a>, for him to form the <i><a href="/wiki/Ordo_Praedicatorum" class="mw-redirect" title="Ordo Praedicatorum">Ordo Praedicatorum</a></i> ("Order of Preachers").
</p><p>In the winter of 1216–1217, at the house of <a href="/wiki/Gregory_IX" class="mw-redirect" title="Gregory IX">Ugolino de' Conti</a>, Dominic first met <a href="/wiki/William_of_Montferrat_(monk)" title="William of Montferrat (monk)">William of Montferrat</a>, who joined Dominic as a friar in the Order of Preachers and remained a close friend.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913137_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913137-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Later_life">Later life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Dominic&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Later life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/Cecilia_Cesarini" title="Cecilia Cesarini">Cecilia Cesarini</a>, who was received by Dominic into his new order, in her old age described him as "...thin and of middle height. His face was handsome and somewhat fair. He had reddish hair and beard and beautiful eyes ... His hands were long and fine and his voice pleasingly resonant. He never got bald, though he wore the full <a href="/wiki/Tonsure" title="Tonsure">tonsure</a>, which was mingled with a few grey hairs."<sup id="cite_ref-canada_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-canada-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Santo_Domingo_en_oraci%C3%B3n_(Boston).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/Santo_Domingo_en_oraci%C3%B3n_%28Boston%29.jpg/220px-Santo_Domingo_en_oraci%C3%B3n_%28Boston%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="277" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/Santo_Domingo_en_oraci%C3%B3n_%28Boston%29.jpg/330px-Santo_Domingo_en_oraci%C3%B3n_%28Boston%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/Santo_Domingo_en_oraci%C3%B3n_%28Boston%29.jpg/440px-Santo_Domingo_en_oraci%C3%B3n_%28Boston%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1746" data-file-height="2195" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Santo_Domingo_en_oraci%C3%B3n_(Boston).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><i>Saint Dominic in prayer</i> by <a href="/wiki/El_Greco" title="El Greco">El Greco</a></div></div></div>
<p>Although he traveled extensively to maintain contact with his growing brotherhood of friars,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913129_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913129-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> Dominic made his headquarters in Rome.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuiraud191391_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuiraud191391-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> In 1219, Pope Honorius III invited Dominic and his companions to take up residence at the ancient Roman <a href="/wiki/Basilica" title="Basilica">basilica</a> of <a href="/wiki/Santa_Sabina" title="Santa Sabina">Santa Sabina</a>, which they did by early 1220. Before that time the friars had only a temporary residence in Rome at the convent of <a href="/wiki/San_Sisto_Vecchio" title="San Sisto Vecchio">San Sisto Vecchio</a>, which Honorius III had given to Dominic circa 1218, intending it to become a convent for a reformation of nuns at Rome under Dominic's guidance. The official foundation of the Dominican convent at <a href="/wiki/Santa_Sabina" title="Santa Sabina">Santa Sabina</a> with its <i>studium conventuale</i>, the first Dominican <i>studium</i> in Rome, occurred with the legal transfer of property from Pope <a href="/wiki/Honorius_III" class="mw-redirect" title="Honorius III">Honorius III</a> to the <a href="/wiki/Dominican_Order" title="Dominican Order">Order of Preachers</a> on 5 June 1222, though the brethren had taken up residence there already in 1220.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> The <i>studium</i> at Santa Sabina was the forerunner of the <i><a href="/wiki/Studium_generale" title="Studium generale">studium generale</a></i> at <a href="/wiki/Santa_Maria_sopra_Minerva" title="Santa Maria sopra Minerva">Santa Maria sopra Minerva</a>. The latter would be transformed in the 16th century into the College of Saint Thomas (<a href="/wiki/Latin_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Latin language">Latin</a>: <i lang="la">Collegium Divi Thomæ</i>), and then in the 20th century into the <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_University_of_Saint_Thomas_Aquinas" title="Pontifical University of Saint Thomas Aquinas">Pontifical University of Saint Thomas Aquinas, <i>Angelicum</i></a> sited at the convent of <a href="/wiki/Santi_Domenico_e_Sisto" title="Santi Domenico e Sisto">Saints Dominic and Sixtus</a>.
</p><p>Dominic arrived in Bologna on 21 December 1218.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913112_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913112-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> A convent was established at the Mascarella church by <a href="/w/index.php?title=Reginald_of_Orleans&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Reginald of Orleans (page does not exist)">Reginald of Orleans</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913111–113_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913111–113-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> Soon afterwards they had to move to the church of San Nicolò of the Vineyards<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913115_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913115-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> Dominic settled in this church and held here the first two General Chapters of the order.(<a href="#CITEREFGuiraud1913">Guiraud 1913</a>, pp.&#160;126, 140)
</p><p>According to Guiraud, Dominic abstained from meat,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913156_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913156-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> "observed stated fasts and periods of silence",<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913116_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913116-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> "selected the worst accommodations and the meanest clothes", and "never allowed himself the luxury of a bed".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913130,_176_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913130,_176-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> "When travelling, he beguiled the journey with spiritual instruction and prayers".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913130–132_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913130–132-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> Guiraud also states that Dominic frequently traveled barefoot and that "rain and other discomforts elicited from his lips nothing but praises to God".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913130_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913130-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Dominic died at the age of fifty-one, according to Guiraud "exhausted with the austerities and labours of his career".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913172_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913172-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> He had reached the convent of <a href="/wiki/St_Nicholas" class="mw-redirect" title="St Nicholas">St Nicholas</a> at <a href="/wiki/Bologna" title="Bologna">Bologna</a>, <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italy</a>, "weary and sick with a fever".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913172_29-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913172-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> Guiraud states that Dominic "made the monks lay him on some sacking stretched upon the ground"<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913172_29-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913172-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> and that "the brief time that remained to him was spent in exhorting his followers to have charity, to guard their humility, and to make their treasure out of poverty".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913173–175_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913173–175-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> He died at noon on 6 August 1221. His body was moved to a simple sarcophagus in 1233.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913175,_181_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913175,_181-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> Under the authority of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Gregory_IX" title="Pope Gregory IX">Pope Gregory IX</a>, Dominic was canonized in 1234. In 1267 Dominic's remains were moved to <a href="/wiki/Arca_di_San_Domenico" title="Arca di San Domenico">the shrine</a>, made by <a href="/wiki/Nicola_Pisano" title="Nicola Pisano">Nicola Pisano</a> and his workshop for the <a href="/wiki/San_Domenico,_Bologna" title="San Domenico, Bologna">Church of St. Dominic in Bologna</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913181_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuiraud1913181-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Dominic is honored in the <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Church_of_England)" title="Calendar of saints (Church of England)">Church of England</a> and in the <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Episcopal_Church)" title="Calendar of saints (Episcopal Church)">Episcopal Church</a> on <a href="/wiki/August_8" title="August 8">8 August</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div style="clear:left;"></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Inquisition">Inquisition</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Dominic&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Inquisition">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pedro_Berruguete_Saint_Dominic_Presiding_over_an_Auto-da-fe_1495.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/45/Pedro_Berruguete_Saint_Dominic_Presiding_over_an_Auto-da-fe_1495.jpg/200px-Pedro_Berruguete_Saint_Dominic_Presiding_over_an_Auto-da-fe_1495.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="328" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/45/Pedro_Berruguete_Saint_Dominic_Presiding_over_an_Auto-da-fe_1495.jpg/300px-Pedro_Berruguete_Saint_Dominic_Presiding_over_an_Auto-da-fe_1495.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/45/Pedro_Berruguete_Saint_Dominic_Presiding_over_an_Auto-da-fe_1495.jpg/400px-Pedro_Berruguete_Saint_Dominic_Presiding_over_an_Auto-da-fe_1495.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1170" data-file-height="1920" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pedro_Berruguete_Saint_Dominic_Presiding_over_an_Auto-da-fe_1495.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><i>St Dominic presiding over an <a href="/wiki/Auto_da_fe" class="mw-redirect" title="Auto da fe">Auto da fe</a></i>, by <a href="/wiki/Pedro_Berruguete" title="Pedro Berruguete">Pedro Berruguete</a>, c. 1495<sup id="cite_ref-Prado_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Prado-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup></div></div></div>
<p>Dominic is commonly but apocryphally associated with the <a href="/wiki/Inquisition" title="Inquisition">Inquisition</a>. Historical sources from Dominic's own time period reveal nothing about his involvement in the Inquisition.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> Dominic died in 1221, and the office of the Inquisition was not established until 1231 in Lombardy and 1234 in Languedoc.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Canon 27 of the <a href="/wiki/Third_Council_of_the_Lateran" title="Third Council of the Lateran">Third Council of the Lateran</a> of 1179 stressed the duty of princes to repress heresy and condemned "the Brabantians, Aragonese, Basques, Navarrese, and others who practice such cruelty toward Christians that they respect neither churches nor monasteries, spare neither widows nor orphans, neither age nor sex, but after the manner of pagans, destroy and lay waste everything".<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> This was followed in 1184 by a <a href="/wiki/Decretal" title="Decretal">decretal</a> of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Lucius_III" title="Pope Lucius III">Pope Lucius III</a>, <i><a href="/wiki/Ad_abolendam" title="Ad abolendam">Ad abolendam</a></i>. This decreed that bishops were to investigate the presence of heresy within their respective dioceses. Practices and procedures of episcopal inquisitions could vary from one diocese to another, depending on the resources available to individual bishops and their relative interest or disinterest. Convinced that Church teaching contained revealed truth, the first recourse of bishops was that of <i>persuasio</i>. Through discourse, debates, and preaching, they sought to present a better explanation of Church teaching. This approach often proved very successful.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1231 Pope Gregory IX appointed a number of Papal Inquisitors, mostly <a href="/wiki/Dominican_Order" title="Dominican Order">Dominicans</a> and <a href="/wiki/Franciscan" class="mw-redirect" title="Franciscan">Franciscans</a>, for the various regions of Europe. As <a href="/wiki/Mendicant" title="Mendicant">mendicants</a>, they were accustomed to travel. Unlike the haphazard episcopal methods, the papal inquisition was thorough and systematic, keeping detailed records. This tribunal or court functioned in France, Italy and parts of Germany and had virtually ceased operation by the early fourteenth century.<sup id="cite_ref-medieval_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-medieval-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the 15th century, the Spanish Inquisition commissioned the artist <a href="/wiki/Pedro_Berruguete" title="Pedro Berruguete">Pedro Berruguete</a> to depict Dominic presiding at an <i><a href="/wiki/Auto_da_f%C3%A9" class="mw-redirect" title="Auto da fé">auto da fé</a></i>. Thus, the Spanish inquisitors promoted a historical legend for the sake of auto-justification.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Reacting against the Spanish tribunals, 16th- and 17th-century Protestant polemicists gladly developed and perpetuated the legend of Dominic the Inquisitor.<sup id="cite_ref-Peters,_Inquisition,_223_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Peters,_Inquisition,_223-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> This image gave German Protestant critics of the Catholic Church an argument against the Dominican Order whose preaching had proven to be a formidable opponent in the lands of the Reformation.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> As Edward Peters notes, "In Protestant historiography of the sixteenth century a kind of anti-cult of St. Dominic grew up."<sup id="cite_ref-Peters,_Inquisition,_223_42-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Peters,_Inquisition,_223-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div style="clear:left;"></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Rosary">Rosary</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Dominic&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Rosary">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Rosary" title="Rosary">Rosary</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Bernardo_Cavallino_-_La_Visione_di_San_Domenico_(anni_1640).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/47/Bernardo_Cavallino_-_La_Visione_di_San_Domenico_%28anni_1640%29.jpg/220px-Bernardo_Cavallino_-_La_Visione_di_San_Domenico_%28anni_1640%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="327" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/47/Bernardo_Cavallino_-_La_Visione_di_San_Domenico_%28anni_1640%29.jpg/330px-Bernardo_Cavallino_-_La_Visione_di_San_Domenico_%28anni_1640%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/47/Bernardo_Cavallino_-_La_Visione_di_San_Domenico_%28anni_1640%29.jpg/440px-Bernardo_Cavallino_-_La_Visione_di_San_Domenico_%28anni_1640%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="511" data-file-height="759" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Bernardo_Cavallino_-_La_Visione_di_San_Domenico_(anni_1640).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><i>The vision of St. Dominic receiving the Rosary from the Virgin</i> by <a href="/wiki/Bernardo_Cavallino" title="Bernardo Cavallino">Bernardo Cavallino</a></div></div></div>
<p>The spread of the <a href="/wiki/Rosary" title="Rosary">Rosary</a>, a Marian devotion, is attributed to the preaching of Dominic.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>  For centuries the Rosary has been at the heart of the <a href="/wiki/Dominican_Order" title="Dominican Order">Dominican Order</a>. <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XI" title="Pope Pius XI">Pope Pius XI</a> stated, "The Rosary of Mary is the principle and foundation on which the very Order of Saint Dominic rests for making perfect the life of its members and obtaining the salvation of others." For centuries, <a href="/wiki/Dominican_Order" title="Dominican Order">Dominicans</a> have been instrumental in spreading the Rosary and emphasizing the Catholic belief in the power of the Rosary.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The feast of Saint Dominic is celebrated with great pomp and devotion in Malta, in the old city of Birgu and the capital city Valletta. The Dominican order has very strong links with Malta and <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_V" title="Pope Pius V">Pope Pius V</a>, a Dominican friar himself, aided the Knights of St. John to build the city of Valletta.<sup id="cite_ref-Rosary_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rosary-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Cord_of_Saint_Dominic">Cord of Saint Dominic</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Dominic&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Cord of Saint Dominic">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Cord (belt) of Saint Dominic is a <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic</a> <a href="/wiki/Sacramental" title="Sacramental">sacramental</a>, which reminds the wearer about the protection of Saint Dominic.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> History of the cord is associated with the miraculous image of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Dominic_in_Soriano" title="Saint Dominic in Soriano">Saint Dominic in Soriano</a>. The length of its strip suits to the perimeter of the painting.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> The beginning of the prayer <i>"O wonderful hope"</i> is placed on the cord.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> According to the tradition, if someone wants to receive grace from Saint Dominic, they should wear it all the time.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> Infertile couples use this cord to prayer for intercession of saint Domninic to get the gift of offspring from God.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Toponymy">Toponymy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Dominic&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Toponymy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Saint Dominic is one of the few historical figures after whom two sovereign countries are named: <a href="/wiki/Dominica" title="Dominica">Dominica</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Dominican_Republic" title="Dominican Republic">Dominican Republic</a>. The capital of the latter, <a href="/wiki/Santo_Domingo" title="Santo Domingo">Santo Domingo</a>, also bears his name.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Veneration">Veneration</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Dominic&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Veneration">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<ul><li>25 January – commemoration of translation of relics to <a href="/wiki/Igreja_de_S%C3%A3o_Roque" title="Igreja de São Roque">Church of Saint Roch</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-heiligenlexikon.de_53-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-heiligenlexikon.de-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>15 February – commemoration of the skull translation (1383),<sup id="cite_ref-Capilla_y_Sepulcro_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Capilla_y_Sepulcro-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-heiligenlexikon.de_53-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-heiligenlexikon.de-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>24 May – commemoration of first translation (1233),<sup id="cite_ref-Zeno_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Zeno-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Capilla_y_Sepulcro_54-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Capilla_y_Sepulcro-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>5 June – commemoration of second translation (1267)<sup id="cite_ref-San_Domenico_di_Guzman_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-San_Domenico_di_Guzman-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>3 July – commemoration of canonization anniversary (1234)<sup id="cite_ref-San_Domenico_di_Guzman_56-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-San_Domenico_di_Guzman-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>13 July – commemoration of canonization anniversary (1234)<sup id="cite_ref-heiligenlexikon.de_53-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-heiligenlexikon.de-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>3 August – main commemoration (Australia)<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>4 August – commemoration by (<a href="/wiki/Traditionalist_Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Traditionalist Catholic">Traditional Roman Catholics</a>),<sup id="cite_ref-Zeno_55-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Zeno-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>5 August – main commemoration (New Zealand)<sup id="cite_ref-heiligenlexikon.de_53-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-heiligenlexikon.de-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>6 August – commemoration of <a href="/wiki/Death_anniversary" title="Death anniversary">death anniversary</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>7 August – main commemoration (<a href="/wiki/Diocese_of_Sosnowiec" class="mw-redirect" title="Diocese of Sosnowiec">Diocese of Sosnowiec</a>, 8 August - anniversary of the dedication of cathedral church)<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>8 August – main commemoration,<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>15 September – commemoration of apparition of <i><a href="/wiki/Saint_Dominic_in_Soriano" title="Saint Dominic in Soriano">Saint Dominic in Soriano</a></i> (traditional date)<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>25 September – commemoration of apparition of <i><a href="/wiki/Saint_Dominic_in_Soriano" title="Saint Dominic in Soriano">Saint Dominic in Soriano</a></i> (modern date)<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>11 November – commemoration of third translation (1411)<sup id="cite_ref-Capilla_y_Sepulcro_54-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Capilla_y_Sepulcro-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>

<li id="cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuiraud191391-19"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuiraud191391_19-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a href="#CITEREFGuiraud1913">Guiraud 1913</a>, p.&#160;91.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-20"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-20">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.domcentral.org/trad/domwork/domwork03.htm">Pierre Mandonnet, O.P. (1948) <i>St. Dominic and His Work</i></a> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20120618034722/http://www.domcentral.org/trad/domwork/domwork03.htm">Archived</a> 18 June 2012 at the <a href="/wiki/Wayback_Machine" title="Wayback Machine">Wayback Machine</a>, Translated by Sister Mary Benedicta Larkin, O.P., B. Herder Book Co., St. Louis/London, Chapt. III, note 50: "If the installation at Santa Sabina does not date from 1220, at least it is from 1221. The official grant was made only in June 1222 (Bullarium O.P., I, 15). But the terms of the bull show that there had been a concession earlier. Before that concession the Pope said that the friars had no hospitium in Rome. At that time St. Sixtus was no longer theirs; Conrad of Metz could not have alluded to St. Sixtus, therefore, when he said in 1221: "the Pope has conferred on them a house in Rome" (Laurent no. 136). It is possible that the Pope was waiting for the completion of the building that he was having done at Santa Sabina, before giving the title to the property, on 5 June 1222, to the new Master of the Order, elected not many days before." Accessed 20 May 2012.</span>
</li>
